# Package net.thevpc.nuts.util

<a id="pkg-net-thevpc-nuts-util"></a>

General-purpose Nuts value types, functional contracts, validation, and utilities.



It contains optional and reference values, predicates and functions, filtering
and matching, tuples, string and memory formatting, assertions and exceptions,
annotations, generators, enums, colors, and common collection/string helpers.
The package is dependency-light and supports the rest of the API.

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-BytePredicate"></a>

## BytePredicate

```java
@NJdkExtension("Missing from java.util.function — JDK only provides IntPredicate, LongPredicate, DoublePredicate")
@FunctionalInterface
public interface BytePredicate
```

**JDK Extension:** Missing from java.util.function — JDK only provides IntPredicate, LongPredicate, DoublePredicate

Represents a predicate (boolean-valued function) of one `byte`-valued argument.



This is the `byte`-specialized version of `java.util.function.Predicate`.




### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `boolean` | [test(byte)](#m-BytePredicate-test) | Evaluates this predicate on the given byte argument. |
| `default BytePredicate` | [and(BytePredicate)](#m-BytePredicate-and) | Returns a composed predicate that represents a short-circuiting logical AND of this predicate and another. |
| `default BytePredicate` | [negate()](#m-BytePredicate-negate) | Returns a predicate that represents the logical negation of this predicate. |
| `default BytePredicate` | [or(BytePredicate)](#m-BytePredicate-or) | Returns a composed predicate that represents a short-circuiting logical OR of this predicate and another. |

### Method Details


---

<a id="m-BytePredicate-test"></a>

#### test

```java
boolean test(byte c)
```

Evaluates this predicate on the given byte argument.

**Parameters:**

- `c` - the input byte

**Returns:**

`true` if the input matches the predicate, otherwise `false`



---

<a id="m-BytePredicate-and"></a>

#### and

```java
default BytePredicate and(BytePredicate other)
```

Returns a composed predicate that represents a short-circuiting logical AND of
this predicate and another. When evaluating the composed predicate, if this
predicate is `false`, then the `other` predicate is not evaluated.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a predicate that will be logically-ANDed with this predicate

**Returns:**

a composed predicate representing the short-circuiting logical AND

**Throws:**

- `NullPointerException` - if `other` is null



---

<a id="m-BytePredicate-negate"></a>

#### negate

```java
default BytePredicate negate()
```

Returns a predicate that represents the logical negation of this predicate.

**Returns:**

a predicate representing the logical negation of this predicate



---

<a id="m-BytePredicate-or"></a>

#### or

```java
default BytePredicate or(BytePredicate other)
```

Returns a composed predicate that represents a short-circuiting logical OR of
this predicate and another. When evaluating the composed predicate, if this
predicate is `true`, then the `other` predicate is not evaluated.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a predicate that will be logically-ORed with this predicate

**Returns:**

a composed predicate representing the short-circuiting logical OR

**Throws:**

- `NullPointerException` - if `other` is null



---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-CharConsumer"></a>

## CharConsumer

```java
@NJdkExtension("Missing from java.util — JDK only provides Consumer<T>")
@FunctionalInterface
public interface CharConsumer
```

**JDK Extension:** Missing from java.util — JDK only provides Consumer<T>

Represents an operation that accepts a single `char`-valued argument and
returns no result. This is the primitive type specialization of `java.util.function.Consumer`
for `char`. Unlike most other functional interfaces, `CharConsumer` is expected
to operate via side-effects.

### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `void` | [accept(char)](#m-CharConsumer-accept) | Performs this operation on the given argument. |
| `default CharConsumer` | [andThen(CharConsumer)](#m-CharConsumer-andThen) | Returns a composed Consumer that performs, in sequence, this operation followed by the after operation. |

### Method Details


---

<a id="m-CharConsumer-accept"></a>

#### accept

```java
void accept(char t)
```

Performs this operation on the given argument.

**Parameters:**

- `t` - the input argument



---

<a id="m-CharConsumer-andThen"></a>

#### andThen

```java
default CharConsumer andThen(CharConsumer after)
```

Returns a composed `Consumer` that performs, in sequence, this
operation followed by the `after` operation. If performing either
operation throws an exception, it is relayed to the caller of the
composed operation.  If performing this operation throws an exception,
the `after` operation will not be performed.

**Parameters:**

- `after` - the operation to perform after this operation

**Returns:**

a composed `Consumer` that performs in sequence this
operation followed by the `after` operation

**Throws:**

- `NullPointerException` - if `after` is null



---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NCharEscape"></a>

## NCharEscape

```java
public interface NCharEscape
```

Encodes a single character into its escaped representation.



This interface is purely about __how__ to encode — it carries no
knowledge of __which__ characters should be escaped or under what
quoting conditions.  That responsibility belongs to [NCharEscapeSet](#cls-NCharEscapeSet).





Implementations are composable via [#andThen](#m-andThen): the first helper
that returns a non-null result wins.



### Fields

| Modifier and Type | Field | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `NCharEscape` | `BACKSLASH` | Backslash prefix: emits \ + c for any character. |
| `NCharEscape` | `BACKSLASH_NAMED` | Standard Java/C named sequences for the six common control characters (\n \r \t \f \b \0). |
| `NCharEscape` | `PERCENT_HEX` | Percent-hex encoding: %XX for every character (unreserved URI characters pass through as null). |
| `NCharEscape` | `UNICODE` | Unicode escape sequences: \0uXXXX for non-printable or non-ASCII characters. |
| `NCharEscape` | `REPEAT` | Repeats the character: emits c + c. |

### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `String` | [escape(char, NStringLiteralFormat)](#m-NCharEscape-escape) | Encodes c inside the context of format. |
| `static NCharEscape` | [ofWrap(String, String)](#m-NCharEscape-ofWrap) | Creates an encoder that wraps c with a fixed prefix and suffix. |
| `default NCharEscape` | [andThen(NCharEscape)](#m-NCharEscape-andThen) | Returns a new encoder that tries this first and falls back to other when this returns null. |
| `static NCharEscape` | [chain(NCharEscape, NCharEscape)](#m-NCharEscape-chain) | Static alias for #andThen, useful in static-import contexts. |

### Method Details


---

#### escape


**Parameters:**

- `c` - the character to encode
- `format` - the surrounding formatter (exposes boundary char, quoteType, …)

**Returns:**

the replacement string, or `null` to pass `c` through unchanged

<a id="m-NCharEscape-ofWrap"></a>

#### ofWrap

```java
static NCharEscape ofWrap(String prefix, String suffix)
```

Creates an encoder that wraps `c` with a fixed `prefix` and
`suffix`.  For example `ofWrap("%", "")` gives percent-prefix
encoding without hex conversion; `ofWrap("&#", ";")` gives XML
decimal char references (when combined with a decimal formatter elsewhere).

**Parameters:**

- `prefix` - 
- `suffix` -

```java
default NCharEscape andThen(NCharEscape other)
```

Returns a new encoder that tries `this` first and falls back to
`other` when `this` returns `null`.

**Parameters:**

- `other` -

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NCharEscapeSet"></a>

## NCharEscapeSet

```java
public  final class NCharEscapeSet
```

Describes __which__ characters must be escaped, __under what quoting
condition__ they must be escaped, and __how__ each group is encoded.



This is entirely separate from the boundary strategy ([NStringLiteralFormatBase.Mode](#cls-NStringLiteralFormatBase-Mode))
and from the boundary-char encoder (`boundaryEscape` on the formatter).
A set is composed of one or more [Entry](#cls-Entry) records, each of which pairs:




- a sorted, deduplicated array of characters (the "trigger set")
- a [When](#cls-When) value: `ALWAYS` or `UNQUOTED_ONLY`
- an [NCharEscape](#cls-NCharEscape) that says how to encode a triggered character





During formatting a character is tested against every entry in order.
The first entry whose trigger set contains the character (binary-search, O(log n))
and whose `when` condition is satisfied determines the encoding.
Characters not found in any entry are passed through unchanged.



<h3>Built-in sets</h3>
```java
// \n \r \t \f \b — always, using named backslash sequences
NCharEscapeSet s = NCharEscapeSet.JAVA_COMMON;

// space — escaped only when no surrounding quotes are present
NCharEscapeSet s = NCharEscapeSet.UNQUOTED_SPACE;

// combine: Java common + unquoted space
NCharEscapeSet s = NCharEscapeSet.combine(NCharEscapeSet.JAVA_COMMON,
                                          NCharEscapeSet.UNQUOTED_SPACE);
```

<h3>Custom entry</h3>
```java
// '#' and '$' must always be backslash-escaped
NCharEscapeSet s = NCharEscapeSet.of(
        NCharEscapeSet.Entry.always("#$", NCharEscape.BACKSLASH));
```

### Fields

| Modifier and Type | Field | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `private final Entry[]` | `entries` |  |
| `static final NCharEscapeSet` | `JAVA_COMMON` | Java/C named backslash sequences for the six common control characters: \n \r \t \f \b \0 — always, regardless of quoting. |
| `static final NCharEscapeSet` | `UNQUOTED_SPACE` | Space must be escaped only when there are no surrounding quotes (bare shell token / unquoted TSON value). |
| `static final NCharEscapeSet` | `UNQUOTED_NEWLINE` | Newline must be escaped only when there are no surrounding quotes. |
| `static final NCharEscapeSet` | `JAVA_WITH_SPACE` | Convenience combination: Java common control chars (always) + space (unquoted only). |

### Constructors

#### `NCharEscapeSet(Entry[] entries)`

```java
private NCharEscapeSet(Entry[] entries)
```

N char escape set.

**Parameters:**

- `entries` - entries

**Returns:**

n char escape set result


### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `static NCharEscapeSet` | [of(Entry)](#m-NCharEscapeSet-of) | Creates a set from one or more entries (order is preserved). |
| `static NCharEscapeSet` | [combine(NCharEscapeSet, NCharEscapeSet)](#m-NCharEscapeSet-combine) | Merges two sets by concatenating their entry lists. |
| `NCharEscapeSet` | [andThen(NCharEscapeSet)](#m-NCharEscapeSet-andThen) | Returns a new set with extra appended at the end. |
| `String` | [apply(char, boolean, NStringLiteralFormat)](#m-NCharEscapeSet-apply) | Looks up c in this set given the current quoting state. |
| `boolean` | [triggers(char, boolean)](#m-NCharEscapeSet-triggers) | Returns true if c is present in any entry whose condition matches quoted. |

#### pickColor

```java
static NColor pickColor(int hashCode)
```

Deterministic mapping from int → NColor.



Two-step mapping ensures colors are evenly chosen across canonical colors,
so grays (which are more numerous) don’t dominate the distribution.

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NEnum"></a>

## NEnum

```java
public interface NEnum
```

implementations of any class T must implement 3 static methods :



- `static T parseLenient(String value)`
- `static T parseLenient(String value, T emptyValue)`
- `static T parseLenient(String value, T emptyValue, T errorValue)`



**Since:** 0.8.1

| :--- | :--- | :--- |

| `String` | [id()](#m-NEnum-id) | Id. |

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NException"></a>

## NException

```java
public class NException extends RuntimeException implements NSessionAwareExceptionBase, NAnyBootAwareExceptionBase
```

Base Nuts Exception. Parent of all Nuts defined Exceptions.

**Author:** thevpc

**Since:** 0.5.4

### Fields

| Modifier and Type | Field | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `private final NSession` | `session` |  |
| `private final NMsg` | `formattedMessage` |  |

### Constructors

#### `NException(NMsg message)`

```java
public NException(NMsg message)
```

Constructs a new runtime exception with the specified detail message. The
cause is not initialized, and may subsequently be initialized by a call
to [#initCause](#m-initCause).

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the detail message. The detail message is saved for later
               retrieval by the [#message()](#m-message) method.


#### `NException(NMsg message, Throwable cause)`

```java
public NException(NMsg message, Throwable cause)
```

Constructs a new runtime exception with the specified detail message and
cause.


Note that the detail message associated with `cause` is __not__
automatically incorporated in this runtime exception's detail message.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the detail message (which is saved for later retrieval by
               the [#message()](#m-message) method).
- `cause` - the cause (which is saved for later retrieval by the
               [#getCause()](#m-getCause) method). (A `null` value is permitted, and
               indicates that the cause is nonexistent or unknown.)


#### `NException(NMsg message, Throwable cause, boolean enableSuppression, boolean writableStackTrace)`

```java
public NException(NMsg message, Throwable cause, boolean enableSuppression, boolean writableStackTrace)
```

Constructs a new runtime exception with the specified detail message,
cause, suppression enabled or disabled, and writable stack trace enabled
or disabled.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the detail message.
- `cause` - the cause. (A `null` value is permitted, and indicates
                          that the cause is nonexistent or unknown.)
- `enableSuppression` - whether suppression is enabled or not
                          disabled
- `writableStackTrace` - whether the stack trace should be writable or not

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NExclude"></a>

## NExclude

```java
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.SOURCE)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.TYPE, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.ANNOTATION_TYPE, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR, ElementType.LOCAL_VARIABLE, ElementType.PACKAGE, ElementType.TYPE_PARAMETER, ElementType.TYPE_USE })
public @interface NExclude
```

Directs Nuts reflection, object mapping, and serialization mechanisms to
ignore and exclude the annotated field or method from property inspection and export.

**Since:** 0.5.4

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NGetter"></a>

## NGetter

```java
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.SOURCE)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR })
public @interface NGetter
```

Marks a method as a property getter (accessor), particularly in fluent or
record-style APIs where getter methods do not follow standard `get...()` or `is...()`
naming conventions. Used by Nuts reflection, object mappers, and serialization.

**Since:** 0.5.4


---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NImmutable"></a>

## NImmutable

```java
@Target(ElementType.TYPE)
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.RUNTIME)
public @interface NImmutable
```

Indicates that instances of the annotated class or interface are immutable
after instantiation, ensuring thread-safety and side-effect-free sharing.

**Since:** 0.5.4


---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NInclude"></a>

## NInclude

```java
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.SOURCE)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.TYPE, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.ANNOTATION_TYPE, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR, ElementType.LOCAL_VARIABLE, ElementType.PACKAGE, ElementType.TYPE_PARAMETER, ElementType.TYPE_USE })
public @interface NInclude
```

Directs Nuts reflection, object mapping, and serialization mechanisms to
explicitly include the annotated field or method as an inspectable property.

**Since:** 0.5.4


---

<a id="m-NNames-pickName"></a>

#### pickName

```java
public static String pickName(int hash, int minCount, int maxCount, NNameFormat format)
```

Picks a name using the given hash, with a number of words between minCount and maxCount (inclusive).



The generated name consists of a combination of adjectives, color names, and personal/fantasy names.
The total number of words in the name will be between `minCount` and `maxCount`, inclusive.
If `maxCount` is less than `minCount`, it will be automatically raised to `minCount`.



The selection is deterministic: the same hash will always produce the same name.



If `format` is `null`, [NNameFormat#TITLE_NAME](#cls-NNameFormat) will be used as the default.






**Examples:**


```java
pickName(12345, 2, 3, NNameFormat.LOWER_CAMEL_CASE) -> "swiftNavyBlueTuring"
pickName(67890, 1, 2, NNameFormat.KEBAB_CASE)     -> "bright-tesla"
pickName(54321, 1, 3, null)                        -> "Curious Red Einstein" // uses TITLE_NAME
```

**Parameters:**

- `hash` - an integer used as a deterministic seed for name selection
- `minCount` - minimum number of words in the resulting name (>= 1, inclusive)
- `maxCount` - maximum number of words in the resulting name (>= minCount, inclusive)
- `format` - the [NNameFormat](#cls-NNameFormat) used to format the resulting name; if `null`, defaults to [NNameFormat#TITLE_NAME](#cls-NNameFormat)

**Returns:**

a deterministic pseudo-random name composed of adjectives, color names, and names

**Throws:**

- `IllegalArgumentException` - if `minCount < 1` or other invalid input



---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NNonNull"></a>

## NNonNull

```java
@NSince(value = "1.0.0")
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.SOURCE)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.TYPE, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.ANNOTATION_TYPE, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR, ElementType.LOCAL_VARIABLE, ElementType.PACKAGE, ElementType.TYPE_PARAMETER, ElementType.TYPE_USE })
public @interface NNonNull
```

**Since:** 1.0.0

Documents that a method return value, parameter, field, or type use
must not be `null`.

**Since:** 1.0.0


---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NNullable"></a>

## NNullable

```java
@NSince(value = "1.0.0")
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.SOURCE)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.TYPE, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.ANNOTATION_TYPE, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR, ElementType.LOCAL_VARIABLE, ElementType.PACKAGE, ElementType.TYPE_PARAMETER, ElementType.TYPE_USE })
public @interface NNullable
```

**Since:** 1.0.0

Documents that a method return value, parameter, field, or type use
may legally be `null`.

<a id="m-NOptional-getDefaultExceptionFactory"></a>

#### getDefaultExceptionFactory

```java
static NOptionalExceptionFactory getDefaultExceptionFactory()
```

return the default ExceptionFactory used for generating exceptions thrown
when NOptional::get fails. When null default implementation falls back to
one of NEmptyOptionalException, NErrorOptionalException (when in
NWorkspace context) NDetachedEmptyOptionalException,
NDetachedErrorOptionalException (when no NWorkspace context can be
resolved)

**Returns:**

default ExceptionFactory



---

<a id="m-NOptional-setDefaultExceptionFactory"></a>

#### setDefaultExceptionFactory

```java
static void setDefaultExceptionFactory(NOptionalExceptionFactory defaultExceptionFactory)
```

set the default ExceptionFactory used for generating exceptions thrown
when NOptional::get fails. When null default implementation falls back to
one of NEmptyOptionalException, NErrorOptionalException (when in
NWorkspace context) NDetachedEmptyOptionalException,
NDetachedErrorOptionalException (when no NWorkspace context can be
resolved)

**Parameters:**

- `defaultExceptionFactory` - 

**Returns:**

default ExceptionFactory

<a id="m-NOptional-ofSingleton"></a>

#### ofSingleton

```java
static <T> NOptional<T> ofSingleton(Collection<T> collection)
```

Creates an NOptional from a collection that must contain exactly one element.



If the collection:
- Is empty: returns empty optional
- Has exactly 1 element: returns NOptional wrapping that element
- Has more than 1 element: returns error optional with message "too many elements"



Use this when you expect exactly one result, and want to catch duplicates as errors.



Example:
```java
List<User> results = db.findByEmail("user@example.com");
  NOptional<User> user = NOptional.ofSingleton(results);
  // Empty if no results, User if exactly 1, Error if >1
</User></User>
```

**Parameters:**

- `collection` - the collection to extract from

**Returns:**

NOptional with the single element, or empty/error based on collection size



---

```java

```

If the collection:
- Is empty: returns empty optional
- Has exactly 1 element: returns NOptional wrapping that element
- Has more than 1 element: returns error optional with message "too many elements"



Use this when you expect exactly one result, and want to catch duplicates as errors.



Example:
```java
List<User> results = db.findByEmail("user@example.com");
  NOptional<User> user = NOptional.ofSingleton(results);
  // Empty if no results, User if exactly 1, Error if >1
</User></User>
```

**Parameters:**

- `collection` - the collection to extract from
- `name` - name a appear in the error message as "missing $name"

**Returns:**

NOptional with the single element, or empty/error based on collection size

Use this when you expect exactly one result, and want to catch duplicates as errors.

```java
List<User> results = db.findByEmail("user@example.com");
```

<a id="m-NOptional-ofNamedFirst"></a>

#### ofNamedFirst

```java
static <T> NOptional<T> ofNamedFirst(Collection<T> collection, String name)
```

Creates an NOptional from the first element of a collection.



If the collection:
- Is empty: returns empty optional
- Has at least 1 element: returns NOptional wrapping the first element



Use this when you only care about the first result and want to ignore the rest.



Example:
```java
List<User> results = db.search("active users");
  NOptional<User> firstActive = NOptional.ofFirst(results);
  // Empty if no results, first User otherwise
</User></User>
```

**Parameters:**

- `collection` - the collection to extract from
- `name` - name a appear in the error message as "missing $name"

**Returns:**

NOptional with the first element, or empty if collection is empty

```java
List<User> results = db.search("active users");
  NOptional<User> firstActive = NOptional.ofFirst(results);

</User></User>
```

```java
List<User> results = db.search("active users");
```

<a id="m-NOptional-ifErrorThrow"></a>

#### ifErrorThrow

```java
default NOptional<T> ifErrorThrow()
```

Throws immediately if this optional is in error state, otherwise returns unchanged.
Useful in chaining to detect and propagate errors early without waiting for terminal operations.



If this optional:
- Is empty: returns this unchanged (empty is not an error)
- Is error: calls [#get()](#m-get) which throws the error
- Is present: returns this unchanged



Use this to fail fast in optional chains before performing expensive operations.



Example:
```java
result = loadConfig()
      .ifErrorThrow()  // Throw now if config load failed
      .map(cfg -> cfg.getDatabase())
      .map(db -> expensiveQuery(db))
      .orNull();
```

**Returns:**

this optional unchanged if not in error state

**Throws:**

- `RuntimeException` - (NErrorOptionalException) if this optional is in error state


- `mapper` - mapper

**Returns:**

flat map result

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIfPresent"></a>

#### mapIfPresent

```java
<V> NOptional<V> mapIfPresent(Function<T,V> mapper)
```

Maps the value if this optional is present (non-empty and non-error).
If the optional is empty or in error state, returns an empty optional of the mapped type.



This is the most common conditional map. Use it when you want to transform
a value only if it exists.

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - function to apply to the value

**Returns:**

mapped optional, or empty if this optional is not present



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIfNotBlank"></a>

#### mapIfNotBlank

```java
<V> NOptional<V> mapIfNotBlank(Function<T,V> mapper)
```

Maps the value if it is not blank according to [NBlankable#isBlank(Object)](#cls-NBlankable).
"Blank" includes:
- null
- Empty strings or strings with only whitespace
- Empty arrays
- Empty collections or maps
- Objects implementing NBlankable where isBlank() returns true



Use this for user-input validation or optional string/collection handling.



Example:
```java
NOptional.of("  ").mapIfNotBlank(NStringUtils::strip);
      // Returns empty (whitespace is blank)
  NOptional.of("hello").mapIfNotBlank(NStringUtils::strip);
      // Returns "hello"
  NOptional.of(new int[0]).mapIfNotBlank(arr -> arr.length);
      // Returns empty (empty array is blank)
```

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - function to apply to the non-blank value

**Returns:**

mapped optional, or empty if value is blank/null/empty/error

**See Also:** NBlankable#isBlank(Object)



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIfNotEmpty"></a>

#### mapIfNotEmpty

```java
<V> NOptional<V> mapIfNotEmpty(Function<T,V> mapper)
```

Maps the value if it is not empty according to [NBlankable#isBlank(Object)](#cls-NBlankable).
This is an alias for [#mapIfNotBlank(Function)](#m-mapIfNotBlank).



"Empty" includes:
- null
- Empty strings or strings with only whitespace
- Empty arrays
- Empty collections or maps
- Objects implementing NBlankable where isBlank() returns true



Use this when you want to explicitly convey that you're checking for "empty" state.



Example:
```java
NOptional.of(Collections.emptyList()).mapIfNotEmpty(List::size);
      // Returns empty (list is empty)
  NOptional.of(List.of(1,2,3)).mapIfNotEmpty(List::size);
      // Returns 3
```

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - function to apply to the non-empty value

**Returns:**

mapped optional, or empty if value is empty/null/error

**See Also:** NBlankable#isBlank(Object)



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIfNotNull"></a>

#### mapIfNotNull

```java
<V> NOptional<V> mapIfNotNull(Function<T,V> mapper)
```

Maps the value if it is not null.
Unlike [#mapIfPresent(Function)](#m-mapIfPresent), this explicitly distinguishes between
a null value (which passes) and an absent/error state (which doesn't).



If this optional is:
- Empty or error: returns empty optional
- Present with null value: mapper is NOT applied, returns empty optional
- Present with non-null value: applies mapper



Useful when you need to treat null as a distinct state from "not present".



Example:
```java
NOptional.ofNullable(null).mapIfNotNull(v -> v.toUpperCase());
      // Returns empty (value is null)
  NOptional.of("hello").mapIfNotNull(v -> v.toUpperCase());
      // Returns "HELLO"
```

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - function to apply to the non-null value

**Returns:**

mapped optional, or empty if value is null, empty, or error



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIf"></a>

#### mapIf

```java
<V> NOptional<V> mapIf(Predicate<T> predicate, Function<T,V> trueExpr, Function<T,V> falseExpr)
```

Conditionally maps this optional based on a predicate.
If the predicate is true, applies `trueExpr`; if false, applies `falseExpr`.



If this optional is empty or in error state, applies `falseExpr` with null value.
Both branches return their results wrapped in NOptional.



Use this for branching logic where both paths should be evaluated and result in a value.



Example:
```java
NOptional<String> status = NOptional.of(user)
      .mapIf(u -> u.getAge() >= 18,
             u -> "Adult: " + u.getName(),           // True branch
             u -> u == null ? "Unknown" : "Minor");  // False branch
</String>
```

**Parameters:**

- `predicate` - the condition to test (null is treated as false)
- `trueExpr` - function to apply if predicate is true
- `falseExpr` - function to apply if predicate is false or optional not present

**Returns:**

NOptional wrapping the result of either trueExpr or falseExpr



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIf"></a>

#### mapIf

```java
NOptional<T> mapIf(Predicate<T> predicate, Function<T,T> trueExpr)
```

Conditionally maps this optional based on a predicate, returning the same type.
If the predicate is true, applies `trueExpr` and returns the transformed value.
If the predicate is false or optional is not present, returns this optional unchanged.



Use this for optional transformations where you only modify the value if a condition holds.



Example:
```java
NOptional<Integer> value = NOptional.of(10)
      .mapIf(v -> v > 5, v -> v * 2)  // Only multiply if > 5
      .ifPresent(v -> log(v));         // Logs 20
</Integer>
```

**Parameters:**

- `predicate` - the condition to test (null is treated as false)
- `trueExpr` - function to apply if predicate is true

**Returns:**

NOptional wrapping the transformed value if predicate passes,
or this optional unchanged if predicate fails or optional not present



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIfNotDefault"></a>

#### mapIfNotDefault

```java
NOptional<T> mapIfNotDefault(Function<T,T> mapper)
```

Maps the value only if it is not the default value (set via [#withDefault(T)](#m-withDefault) or [#withDefault(Supplier)](#m-withDefault)).
If this optional is:
- Empty or error: returns unchanged
- Present with actual value (not default): applies mapper
- Present but equals the default: returns unchanged



Use this to apply transformations only to user-provided values, not defaults.

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - function to apply to non-default values

**Returns:**

NOptional with mapper applied if value is not the default, unchanged otherwise



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIfDefault"></a>

#### mapIfDefault

```java
NOptional<T> mapIfDefault(Function<T,T> mapper)
```

Maps the value only if it is the default value (set via [#withDefault(T)](#m-withDefault) or [#withDefault(Supplier)](#m-withDefault)).
If this optional is:
- Empty or error: returns unchanged
- Present but not default: returns unchanged
- Present and equals the default: applies mapper



Use this to apply special handling to default values.

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - function to apply to default values

**Returns:**

NOptional with mapper applied if value is the default, unchanged otherwise



---

<a id="m-NOptional-mapIfNotError"></a>

#### mapIfNotError

```java
<V> NOptional<V> mapIfNotError(Function<T,V> mapper)
```

Map if not error.

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - mapper

**Returns:**

map if not error result



---

<a id="m-NOptional-map"></a>

#### map

```java
<V> NOptional<V> map(Function<T,V> mapper)
```

Map.

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - mapper

**Returns:**

map result



---

<a id="m-NOptional-instanceOf"></a>

#### instanceOf

```java
<V> NOptional<V> instanceOf(Class<V> targetClass)
```

Casts this optional's value to a specific type if the value is an instance of that type.
If the value is not an instance of the target class, returns an empty optional.



Use this for safe type casting without exceptions.



Example:
```java
NOptional<Object> obj = NOptional.of(getSomeObject());
  NOptional<String> str = obj.instanceOf(String.class);
      // Returns wrapped String if obj is a String, empty otherwise

  String value = str.orNull(); // null if not a String
</String></Object>
```

**Parameters:**

- `targetClass` - the class to check and cast to

**Returns:**

NOptional wrapping the casted value if instance of targetClass,
empty optional otherwise



---

<a id="m-NOptional-then"></a>

#### then

```java
<V> NOptional<V> then(Function<T,V> mapper)
```

Transforms this optional's value and wraps the result back into an NOptional.
Enables safe chaining of non-optional method calls to handle deep object graphs.



If this optional is:
- Not present or error: returns an empty optional
- Present: applies mapper and wraps result in NOptional (result can be null)



Use this for the common pattern: `a?.b()?.c()?.d`



Example (safe null traversal):
```java
String path = NOptional.of(clazz.getProtectionDomain())
      .then(x -> x.getCodeSource())           // May return null
      .then(x -> x.getLocation())              // May return null
      .then(x -> x.getPath())                  // May return null
      .orNull(); // Gracefully returns null if any step is null
```

**Parameters:**

- `mapper` - function that takes the value and returns a non-optional result

**Returns:**

NOptional wrapping the mapper result, or empty if this optional is not present



---

<a id="m-NOptional-filter"></a>

#### filter

```java
NOptional<T> filter(NMessagedPredicate<T> predicate)
```

Filters this optional using a predicate that carries its own error message.
An NMessagedPredicate combines a `Predicate` and an associated error message,
allowing the predicate logic itself to define the failure reason.



Use this when the validation logic naturally knows why it failed.

**Parameters:**

- `predicate` - a messaged predicate (predicate + error message combined)

**Returns:**

this optional unchanged if predicate passes or optional is not present,
empty optional with predicate's message if predicate fails



---

<a id="m-NOptional-filter"></a>

#### filter

```java
NOptional<T> filter(Predicate<T> predicate)
```

Filters this optional based on a predicate.
If the value is present and passes the predicate, returns this optional unchanged.
If the value is present but fails the predicate, returns an empty optional.
If this optional is empty or in error state, returns this optional unchanged.



Use this to add additional validation or constraints to an existing optional.



Example:
```java
NOptional.of(user)
      .filter(u -> u.getAge() >= 18) // Passes if age >= 18
      .get(); // Throws NEmptyOptionalException if age check failed
```

**Parameters:**

- `predicate` - the condition to test

**Returns:**

this optional unchanged if predicate passes or optional is not present,
empty optional if predicate fails



---

<a id="m-NOptional-filter"></a>

#### filter

```java
NOptional<T> filter(Predicate<T> predicate, Supplier<NMsg> message)
```

Filters this optional with a custom error message if the predicate fails.
Semantically equivalent to [#filter(Predicate)](#m-filter), but allows you to provide
a descriptive message that will be included in the exception if [#get()](#m-get) is called.



Example:
```java
NOptional.of(user)
      .filter(u -> u.getAge() >= 18,
              () -> NMsg.ofC("User must be 18 or older, got %d", user.getAge()))
      .get(); // Throws with custom message if age check fails
```

**Parameters:**

- `predicate` - the condition to test
- `message` - supplier for custom error message if predicate fails

**Returns:**

this optional unchanged if predicate passes or optional is not present,
empty optional with custom message if predicate fails



---

<a id="m-NOptional-ifPresent"></a>

#### ifPresent

```java
NOptional<T> ifPresent(Consumer<T> action)
```

Executes a side-effect operation if this optional is present (non-empty and non-error).
Returns this optional unchanged for chaining.



Use this to perform actions based on a present value without transforming it.



Example:
```java
NOptional.of(user)
      .ifPresent(u -> logger.info("User: {}", u.getName()))
      .map(u -> u.getEmail())
      .ifPresent(email -> sendNotification(email));
```

**Parameters:**

- `action` - consumer function to execute on the value

**Returns:**

this optional unchanged, for chaining



---

#### ifCondition

```java
NOptional<T> ifCondition(Predicate<NOptional<T>> condition, Consumer<NOptional<T>> action)
```

If condition.

**Parameters:**

- `condition` - condition
- `action` - action

**Returns:**

if condition result



---

#### ifNonPresent

```java
NOptional<T> ifNonPresent(Runnable action)
```

If non present.

**Parameters:**

- `action` - action

**Returns:**

if non present result



---

#### ifNull

```java
NOptional<T> ifNull(Runnable action)
```

If null.

**Parameters:**

if null result



---

```java
NOptional<T> ifError(Consumer<Throwable> action)
```

---

<a id="m-NOptional-orElse"></a>

#### orElse

```java
T orElse(T other)
```

Returns the value if present, otherwise returns `other`.
If this optional is in an error state, it returns `other`.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - the value to be returned if this optional is empty or error

**Returns:**

the value, or `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orElseGetOptionalOf"></a>

#### orElseGetOptionalOf

```java
NOptional<T> orElseGetOptionalOf(Supplier<T> other)
```

Returns this optional if present, otherwise returns a new optional containing
the result of invoking `other`.
If the result of `other` is `null`, an empty optional is returned.
If this optional is in an error state, it returns a new optional containing
the result of invoking `other`.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a `Supplier` whose result is returned in a new NOptional if this one is empty or error

**Returns:**

this optional if present, or a new NOptional with the result of `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orElseGetOptionalFrom"></a>

#### orElseGetOptionalFrom

```java
NOptional<T> orElseGetOptionalFrom(Supplier<NOptional<T>> other)
```

Returns this optional if present, otherwise returns the optional provided by the supplier.
This handles both **empty** and **error** states by attempting to provide a fallback optional.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a `Supplier` that returns an alternative `NOptional`

**Returns:**

this optional if present, otherwise the optional provided by `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orElseGet"></a>

#### orElseGet

```java
T orElseGet(Supplier<? extends T> other)
```

Returns the value if present, otherwise invokes `other` and returns the result.
If this optional is in an error state, it invokes `other` and returns the result.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a `Supplier` whose result is returned if this optional is empty or error

**Returns:**

the value, or the result of `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orElseThrow"></a>

#### orElseThrow

```java
<R extends Throwable> T orElseThrow(Supplier<? extends R> exceptionSupplier) throws R
```

Returns the contained value if present, otherwise throws an exception produced by the exception supplier.
If this optional is in an error state, it throws an exception produced by its internal error mechanism
(the default ExceptionFactory).

**Parameters:**

- `exceptionSupplier` - the supplier that will return the exception to be thrown

**Returns:**

the value

**Throws:**

- `R` - if this optional is empty
- `RuntimeException` - if this optional is in an error state (throws via [#get()](#m-get))



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onEmpty"></a>

#### onEmpty

```java
NOptional<T> onEmpty(T other)
```

Returns this optional if present or in an error state, otherwise returns a new optional
containing the provided value `other`.
This method specifically handles the **empty** state.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - the value to return in a new NOptional if this one is empty

**Returns:**

this optional if present or error, or a new NOptional with the value `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-ifEmptyUse"></a>

#### ifEmptyUse

```java
NOptional<T> ifEmptyUse(Supplier<NOptional<T>> other)
```

Returns this optional if present or in an error state, otherwise returns the optional provided by the supplier.
This method specifically handles the **empty** state.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a `Supplier` that returns an alternative `NOptional`

**Returns:**

this optional if present or error, otherwise the optional provided by `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-get"></a>

#### get

```java
T get()
```

Returns the contained value if present.



If this optional is:
- Present: returns the value.
- Empty: throws `NEmptyOptionalException`.
- Error: throws `NErrorOptionalException` (or another exception type based on the configured `ExceptionFactory`).



This is the "active" accessor that forces a result or an exception.

**Returns:**

the value

**Throws:**

- `RuntimeException` - if empty or error

**See Also:** #orNull(), #orDefault()



---

<a id="m-NOptional-get"></a>

#### get

```java
T get(Supplier<NMsg> message)
```

Returns the contained value if present, using a custom message for the exception if empty.



If this optional is:
- Present: returns the value.
- Empty: throws `NEmptyOptionalException` using the provided message.
- Error: throws `NErrorOptionalException` (or another exception type) using its existing error message.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - custom message supplier for the exception if empty

**Returns:**

the value

**Throws:**

- `RuntimeException` - if empty or error



---

<a id="m-NOptional-getError"></a>

#### getError

```java
Throwable getError()
```

Returns the `Throwable` that caused this optional to be in an error state.
Returns `null` if this optional is not in an error state (i.e., it is present or empty).

**Returns:**

the underlying `Throwable` or `null`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-getExceptionFactory"></a>

#### getExceptionFactory

```java
NOptionalExceptionFactory getExceptionFactory()
```

Returns the `ExceptionFactory` associated with this optional instance.
This factory is used to generate exceptions when [#get()](#m-get) fails.

**Returns:**

the configured `ExceptionFactory`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orNull"></a>

#### orNull

```java
T orNull()
```

Returns the contained value, or `null` if this optional is empty or an error.
This is the "passive" accessor — it never throws an exception.



Use this when:
- You want to gracefully handle missing values without exceptions
- You're chaining multiple nullable operations



Example:
```java
String path = NOptional.of(clazz.getProtectionDomain())
      .then(x -> x.getCodeSource())
      .then(x -> x.getLocation())
      .then(x -> x.getPath())
      .orNull(); // Returns null if any step returns null
```

**Returns:**

the value, or null if empty/error



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orDefault"></a>

#### orDefault

```java
T orDefault()
```

Returns the contained value, or the configured default value if this optional is empty.
The default must be explicitly set using [#withDefault(T)](#m-withDefault) or [#withDefault(Supplier)](#m-withDefault).



If this optional is:
- Present: returns the value
- Empty with default configured: returns the default
- Empty without default: throws NEmptyOptionalException
- Error: throws NErrorOptionalException



Use this when:
- You've explicitly configured a fallback via `withDefault()`
- You want to provide sensible defaults (e.g., 0 for numeric types, empty collections, etc.)



Example:
```java
// In config class:
  public NOptional<NFetchStrategy> getFetchStrategy() {
      return NOptional.ofNamed(strategy, "fetchStrategy")
          .withDefault(() -> NFetchStrategy.ONLINE); // Default if not set
  }

  // Usage:
  if (session.getFetchStrategy().orDefault() != NFetchStrategy.OFFLINE) {
      // Use the strategy or the default
  }
</NFetchStrategy>
```

**Returns:**

the value, the configured default, or throws if neither exists

**Throws:**

- `NEmptyOptionalException` - if empty with no default
- `NErrorOptionalException` - if in error state

**See Also:** #withDefault(T), #withDefault(Supplier)



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orDefault"></a>

#### orDefault

```java
T orDefault(Class<T> defaultType)
```

Returns the contained value, or the configured default value if this optional is empty.



If no explicit default has been configured (via [#withDefault(T)](#m-withDefault) or
[#withDefault(Supplier)](#m-withDefault)), this method falls back to the JVM default value
for the given `defaultType`.



The default resolution follows these steps:


- If this optional is present: returns the value
- If empty with a configured default: resolves the default recursively
- If the resolved default is `null` or no default is configured:
      returns [net.thevpc.nuts.reflect.NReflectUtils#getDefaultValue(Class)](#cls-net-thevpc-nuts-reflect-NReflectUtils) for `defaultType`





If this optional is in an error state, the error is propagated.



This method is useful when a type-level default is acceptable as a final fallback
(for example `0` for numeric types, `false` for booleans, or `null`
for reference types), without requiring an explicit `withDefault()` configuration.



Example:
```java
int timeout = config.getTimeout().orDefault(int.class);
  boolean enabled = flags.getEnabled().orDefault(boolean.class);
```

**Parameters:**

- `defaultType` - the type whose JVM default value is used as a final fallback;
                   may be `null`, in which case `null` is returned

**Returns:**

the value, the configured default, or the JVM default for `defaultType`

**Throws:**

- `NErrorOptionalException` - if in error state

**See Also:** #orDefault(), #withDefault(T), #withDefault(Supplier)



---

<a id="m-NOptional-orDefaultOptional"></a>

#### orDefaultOptional

```java
NOptional<T> orDefaultOptional()
```

Wraps the result of [#orDefault()](#m-orDefault) back into an NOptional.
If this optional is present, returns an NOptional wrapping the value.
If this optional is empty but has a default configured, returns an NOptional wrapping the default.
If this optional is empty without a default **or is in an error state**, returns an empty NOptional.



Useful for chaining operations that expect NOptional return types.



Example:
```java
NOptional<Boolean> isBot = NOptional.ofNamed(bot, "bot")
.withDefault(() -> workspace.getBootOptions().getBot().orElse(false));

// Later, use orDefaultOptional() to re-wrap for further chaining.
// If the original optional was an Error, this will return Empty.
NOptional<String> description = isBot.orDefaultOptional()
.map(b -> b ? "Bot mode" : "Interactive mode");
</String></Boolean>
```

**Returns:**

NOptional containing the value or default, or empty if neither exists or the optional is an Error

**See Also:** #orDefault()



---

<a id="m-NOptional-ifEmptyNull"></a>

#### ifEmptyNull

```java
NOptional<T> ifEmptyNull()
```

Returns an empty optional if this optional is empty, otherwise returns this optional unchanged.
This method is useful primarily for documentation or for consistency in chains.

**Returns:**

this optional if present or error, otherwise an empty optional



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onBlank"></a>

#### onBlank

```java
NOptional<T> onBlank(T other)
```

Returns this optional if its value is not blank (according to [NBlankable#isBlank(Object)](#cls-NBlankable)),
otherwise returns a new optional containing the provided value `other`.
The blank check applies only if the optional is **present**.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - the value to return in a new NOptional if this one is present but blank

**Returns:**

this optional if present and not blank, or a new NOptional with the value `other`

**See Also:** #mapIfNotBlank(Function)



---


Returns this optional if its value is not blank, otherwise returns an empty optional
with a custom empty message.
The blank check applies only if the optional is **present**.

**Parameters:**

- `emptyMessage` - supplier for the empty message if the present value is blank

**Returns:**

this optional if present and not blank, otherwise an empty optional



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onBlankEmpty"></a>

#### onBlankEmpty

```java
NOptional<T> onBlankEmpty()
```

Returns this optional if its value is not blank, otherwise returns an empty optional
with the default empty message.
The blank check applies only if the optional is **present**.

**Returns:**

this optional if present and not blank, otherwise an empty optional



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onErrorEmpty"></a>

#### onErrorEmpty

```java
NOptional<T> onErrorEmpty()
```

Returns an empty optional if this optional is in an error state, otherwise returns this optional unchanged.
This method effectively converts an error state into a non-throwing empty state.

**Returns:**

this optional if present or empty, otherwise an empty optional



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onError"></a>

#### onError

```java
NOptional<T> onError(T other)
```

Returns this optional if present or empty, otherwise returns a new optional
containing the provided value `other`.
This method specifically handles the **error** state.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - the value to return in a new NOptional if this one is in error state

**Returns:**

this optional if present or empty, or a new NOptional with the value `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onBlankUse"></a>

#### onBlankUse

```java
NOptional<T> onBlankUse(Supplier<NOptional<T>> other)
```

Returns this optional if its value is not blank, otherwise returns the optional provided by the supplier.
The blank check applies only if the optional is **present**.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a `Supplier` that returns an alternative `NOptional`

**Returns:**

this optional if present and not blank, otherwise the optional provided by `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onNullUse"></a>

#### onNullUse

```java
NOptional<T> onNullUse(Supplier<NOptional<T>> other)
```

Returns this optional if its value is not `null`, otherwise returns the optional provided by the supplier.
The null check applies only if the optional is **present**.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a `Supplier` that returns an alternative `NOptional`

**Returns:**

this optional if present and not null, otherwise the optional provided by `other`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onNullEmpty"></a>

#### onNullEmpty

```java
NOptional<T> onNullEmpty()
```

Returns an empty optional if this optional is present but holds a `null` value,
otherwise returns this optional unchanged.



If this optional is:


- Present with non-null value: returns this unchanged.
- Present with `null` value: returns a new empty optional.
- Empty or Error: returns this unchanged.





Use this to explicitly treat a present, but null, value as a non-present (empty) state.

**Returns:**

this optional if non-null, or a new empty optional if present and null.



---

<a id="m-NOptional-onErrorUse"></a>

#### onErrorUse

```java
NOptional<T> onErrorUse(Supplier<NOptional<T>> other)
```

Returns this optional if it is present or empty, otherwise returns the optional provided by the supplier.
This method specifically handles the **error** state by providing a recovery path.



If this optional is:


- Present or Empty: returns this unchanged.
- Error: returns the `NOptional` result from `other`.





Use this when an operation might fail (return an error optional) but a reliable fallback optional is available.

**Parameters:**

- `other` - a `Supplier` that returns an alternative `NOptional`

**Returns:**

this optional if present or empty, otherwise the optional provided by `other`



---

```java
boolean isEmpty()
```

true when not an error and has no content

return true if this is valid null value. `isPresent()` would return

return true if this is neither error nor empty value.

true if this is neither error nor empty value

return true if this is either error or empty value.

true if this is either error or empty value

<a id="m-NOptional-type"></a>

#### type

```java
NOptionalType type()
```

Returns the current state type of this optional, which can be:


- `PRESENT`: Value is available and not an error.
- `EMPTY`: Value is absent (e.g., null, not found).
- `ERROR`: An evaluation failed or an error state was explicitly set.

**Returns:**

the `NOptionalType` representing the state of this optional



---

<a id="m-NOptional-message"></a>

#### message

```java
Supplier<NMsg> message()
```

Returns the message associated with this optional.


- If **Empty**: Returns the empty message supplier.
- If **Error**: Returns the error message supplier.
- If **Present**: Returns `null`.





The supplier must be invoked to get the actual message (an `NMsg` instance).

**Returns:**

a `Supplier` for the associated `NMsg`, or `null` if present.



---

<a id="m-NOptional-withMessage"></a>

#### withMessage

```java
NOptional<T> withMessage(Supplier<NMsg> message)
```

Sets a custom error message for this optional.
The message will be used when [#get()](#m-get) is called and the optional is empty or error.



Use this to provide a full, specific error message for debugging or user communication.



Example:
```java
NOptional.ofEmpty()
      .withMessage(() -> NMsg.ofC("User ID %d not found in database", userId))
      .get(); // Throws with custom message
```

**Parameters:**

- `message` - supplier for the full error message

**Returns:**

this optional with custom message, for chaining



---

<a id="m-NOptional-withMessage"></a>

#### withMessage

```java
NOptional<T> withMessage(NMsg message)
```

Sets a custom error message for this optional.
The message will be used when [#get()](#m-get) is called and the optional is empty or error.



Use this to provide a full, specific error message for debugging or user communication.



Example:
```java
NOptional.ofEmpty()
      .withMessage(() -> NMsg.ofC("User ID %d not found in database", userId))
      .get(); // Throws with custom message
```

**Parameters:**

- `message` - for the full error message

**Returns:**

this optional with custom message, for chaining



---

<a id="m-NOptional-withName"></a>

#### withName

```java
NOptional<T> withName(NMsg name)
```

Sets a name for this optional, used to construct an automatic error message.
The message will be formatted as "missing {name}" when [#get()](#m-get) is called.



Use this as a shorthand when you want a consistent, auto-generated message format.



Example:
```java
NOptional.ofEmpty()
      .withName("email address")
      .get(); // Throws with message "missing email address"
```

**Parameters:**

- `name` - NMsg representing the name

**Returns:**

this optional with automatic message "missing {name}", for chaining



---

<a id="m-NOptional-withName"></a>

#### withName

```java
NOptional<T> withName(String name)
```

Sets a name for this optional, used to construct an automatic error message.
The message will be formatted as "missing {name}" when [#get()](#m-get) is called.



Use this as a shorthand when you want a consistent, auto-generated message format.



Example:
```java
NOptional.ofEmpty()
      .withName("user email")
      .get(); // Throws with message "missing user email"
```

**Parameters:**

- `name` - string representing the name

**Returns:**

this optional with automatic message "missing {name}", for chaining

#### withExceptionFactory


Returns a new `NOptional` based on this one, but configured to use the provided
`ExceptionFactory` for generating exceptions when terminal methods like [#get()](#m-get) are called.

- `exceptionFactory` - the factory to use for creating exceptions

---

<a id="m-NOptional-asOptional"></a>

#### asOptional

```java
Optional<T> asOptional()
```

Converts this `NOptional` to a standard Java `Optional<T>`.



If this optional is:


- Present (including present-with-null): returns a present `Optional` wrapping the value.
- Empty: returns an empty `Optional`.
- Error: returns an empty `Optional` (to remain coherent with Java's `Optional` which does not support an error state).





**No exception is thrown.**

**Returns:**

a standard Java `Optional`



---

<a id="m-NOptional-stream"></a>

#### stream

```java
NStream<T> stream()
```

Returns a sequential `NStream` with the contained value if this optional is present.



If this optional is:


- Present: returns a stream containing only the value.
- Empty: returns an empty stream.
- Error: returns an empty stream (to avoid throwing an exception).

**Returns:**

a sequential `NStream`



---

```java
Stream<T> jstream()
```

Jstream.

**Returns:**

jstream result

Or false.

or false result

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NOptionalErrorException"></a>

## NOptionalErrorException

```java
public class NOptionalErrorException extends NException
```

Generic exception to be thrown when an optional is erroneous is not found.

**Author:** thevpc

**Since:** 0.5.4

### Constructors

#### `NOptionalErrorException(NMsg message)`

```java
public NOptionalErrorException(NMsg message)
```

Constructs a new runtime exception with the specified detail message.
The cause is not initialized, and may subsequently be initialized by a
call to [#initCause](#m-initCause).

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the detail message. The detail message is saved for
               later retrieval by the [#message()](#m-message) method.


#### `NOptionalErrorException(NMsg message, Throwable cause)`

```java
public NOptionalErrorException(NMsg message, Throwable cause)
```

Constructs a new runtime exception with the specified detail message and
cause.


Note that the detail message associated with
`cause` is __not__ automatically incorporated in
this runtime exception's detail message.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the detail message (which is saved for later retrieval
               by the [#message()](#m-message) method).
- `cause` - the cause (which is saved for later retrieval by the
               [#getCause()](#m-getCause) method).  (A `null` value is
               permitted, and indicates that the cause is nonexistent or
               unknown.)


#### `NOptionalErrorException(NMsg message, Throwable cause, boolean enableSuppression, boolean writableStackTrace)`

```java
public NOptionalErrorException(NMsg message, Throwable cause, boolean enableSuppression, boolean writableStackTrace)
```

Constructs a new runtime exception with the specified detail
message, cause, suppression enabled or disabled, and writable
stack trace enabled or disabled.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the detail message.
- `cause` - the cause.  (A `null` value is permitted,
                          and indicates that the cause is nonexistent or unknown.)
- `enableSuppression` - whether or not suppression is enabled
                          or disabled
- `writableStackTrace` - whether or not the stack trace should
                          be writable



---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NOptionalExceptionFactory"></a>

## NOptionalExceptionFactory

```java
public interface NOptionalExceptionFactory
```

Factory interface used to customize the creation of exceptions thrown by
`NOptional` when terminal methods (like [NOptional#get()](#cls-NOptional))
encounter a failure condition (Empty or Error), as well as exceptions
thrown by related utility classes (like assertion helpers).

### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `RuntimeException` | [createOptionalEmptyException(NMsg)](#m-NOptionalExceptionFactory-createOptionalEmptyException) | Creates a RuntimeException to be thrown when a value is expected, but the NOptional is in the **Empty** state. |
| `RuntimeException` | [createOptionalErrorException(NMsg, Throwable)](#m-NOptionalExceptionFactory-createOptionalErrorException) | Creates a RuntimeException to be thrown when a terminal method is called on an optional that is in the **Error** state. |
| `RuntimeException` | [createAssertException(NMsg, Throwable)](#m-NOptionalExceptionFactory-createAssertException) | Creates a RuntimeException to be thrown for general **assertion failures** within the Nuts framework (e.g., by the NAssert utility). |
| `RuntimeException` | [createCmdLineException(NMsg, Throwable)](#m-NOptionalExceptionFactory-createCmdLineException) | Creates a RuntimeException specifically for **command-line related errors**, typically encountered during option parsing, validation, or command execution. |

### Method Details


---


Creates a `RuntimeException` to be thrown when a value is expected,
but the [NOptional](#cls-NOptional) is in the **Empty** state.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the descriptive message for the empty state

**Returns:**

the runtime exception to throw (e.g., NEmptyOptionalException)


Creates a `RuntimeException` to be thrown when a terminal method
is called on an optional that is in the **Error** state.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the descriptive error message
- `e` - the underlying throwable that caused the error state, if available (may be null)

**Returns:**

the runtime exception to throw (e.g., NErrorOptionalException)


within the Nuts framework (e.g., by the `NAssert` utility).

- `e` - the underlying throwable, if available (may be null)


Creates a `RuntimeException` specifically for **command-line related errors**,
typically encountered during option parsing, validation, or command execution.

**Parameters:**

- `message` - the command-line error message
- `e` - the underlying throwable, if available (may be null)

**Returns:**

the runtime exception to throw

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NOptionalType"></a>

## NOptionalType

```java
public enum NOptionalType implements NEnum
```

Defines the possible states of an [NOptional](#cls-NOptional) instance.
Unlike Java's `Optional` (which is bi-state: absent or present),
`NOptional` is tri-state:


- [#EMPTY](#m-EMPTY): The value is logically absent (e.g., not found, not set).
- [#PRESENT](#m-PRESENT): A value is held by the optional (which may itself be `null`).
- [#ERROR](#m-ERROR): A technical or logical failure occurred during evaluation.



**Author:** vpc

### Enum Constants

| Constant | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `EMPTY` | Indicates that the optional holds no value. |
| `PRESENT` | Indicates that the optional holds a valid value. |
| `ERROR` | Indicates that the optional is in a failure state, meaning an attempt to retrieve the value resulted in an error (e.g., an exception during a lazy... |

### Fields

| Modifier and Type | Field | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `private final String` | `id` | lower-cased identifier for the enum entry |

### Constructors

#### `NOptionalType()`

```java
NOptionalType()
```

default constructor


### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `static NOptional&lt;NOptionalType&gt;` | [parse(String)](#m-NOptionalType-parse) | Parses the given string value into an NOptional. |
| `String` | [id()](#m-NOptionalType-id) | Returns the lower-cased identifier for the enum entry. |

### Method Details


---

**Returns:**

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NRenamed"></a>

## NRenamed

```java
@NSince(value = "1.0.0")
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.RUNTIME)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.TYPE, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.ANNOTATION_TYPE, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR, ElementType.LOCAL_VARIABLE, ElementType.PACKAGE, ElementType.TYPE_PARAMETER, ElementType.TYPE_USE })
public @interface NRenamed
```

**Since:** 1.0.0

Documents that an API element (type, method, parameter, constructor, etc.)
was renamed from a previous name.

**Since:** 1.0.0


---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NSetter"></a>

## NSetter

```java
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.SOURCE)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR })
public @interface NSetter
```

Marks a method as a property setter (mutator), particularly in fluent or
builder-style APIs where setter methods do not follow standard `set...()`
naming conventions. Used by Nuts reflection, object mappers, and serialization.

**Since:** 0.5.4


---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NStringLiteralFormatBase"></a>

## NStringLiteralFormatBase

```java
public  abstract class NStringLiteralFormatBase implements NStringLiteralFormat
```

Composable string literal formatter.



Three orthogonal concerns are kept separate:




- **Boundary strategy** ([Mode](#cls-Mode)) — how the surrounding delimiters
      work: backslash-escape, repeat-char, or per-line prefix.
- **Boundary encoder** (`boundaryEscape: NCharEscape`) — __how__
      to encode the boundary character when it appears in the body.
      [NCharEscape#BACKSLASH](#cls-NCharEscape) for `\"`, [NCharEscape#REPEAT](#cls-NCharEscape) for
      SQL `''`, or any custom encoder.
- **Body escape set** (`charEscapeSet: NCharEscapeSet`) — __which__
      other characters need encoding, under what quoting condition, and how
      each group is encoded.





No backslash is hardcoded anywhere in this class.



<h3>Examples</h3>
```java
// Standard Java double-quoted string
NStringLiteralFormat f = NStringLiteralFormat.ofDoubleQuoted(NSupportMode.IF_NEEDED);
f.format("hello world");    // → "hello world"
f.format("simple");         // → simple

// SQL single-quoted, repeat boundary
NStringLiteralFormat sql = NStringLiteralFormat.ofRepeatChar('\'',
        NSupportMode.ALWAYS, NCharEscapeSet.JAVA_COMMON, null);
sql.format("it's\nfine");   // → 'it''s\nfine'

// ¶ line-string, custom escape char for boundary
NStringLiteralFormat para = NStringLiteralFormat.builder()
        .mode(NStringLiteralFormat.Mode.PREFIX)
        .linePrefix("¶ ").lineSuffix("\n")
        .charEscapeSet(NCharEscapeSet.JAVA_COMMON)
        .condition(NSupportMode.ALWAYS)
        .build();
para.format("hello\nworld"); // → ¶ hello\n\n¶ world\n  (split on real \n)

// URL-encoded, no boundary
NStringLiteralFormat url = NStringLiteralFormat.builder()
        .mode(NStringLiteralFormat.Mode.PREFIX)
        .linePrefix("").lineSuffix("")
        .charEscapeSet(NCharEscapeSet.of(
                NCharEscapeSet.Entry.always(
                        " \n\r\t!#$&'()*+,/:;=?@[]",
                        NCharEscape.PERCENT_HEX)))
        .condition(NSupportMode.ALWAYS)
        .build();
url.format("hello world");   // → hello%20world
```

| `protected final NElementType` | `quoteType` |  |
| `protected final NSupportMode` | `condition` |  |
| `protected final NCharEscape` | `boundaryEscape` |  |
| `protected final NCharEscapeSet` | `charEscapeSet` |  |

<a id="m-NStringLiteralFormatBase-ofEscapeChar"></a>

#### ofEscapeChar

```java
public static NStringLiteralFormatBase ofEscapeChar(NElementType quoteType, NSupportMode condition, NCharEscapeSet charEscapeSet, NCharEscape boundaryEscape)
```

Backslash-escape strategy.

**Parameters:**

- `quoteType` - target literal syntax
- `condition` - when to add surrounding boundary characters
- `charEscapeSet` - which additional chars to escape and how (null = none)
- `boundaryEscape` - how to encode the boundary char (null → [NCharEscape#BACKSLASH](#cls-NCharEscape))

---

<a id="m-NStringLiteralFormatBase-ofRepeatChar"></a>

#### ofRepeatChar

```java
public static NStringLiteralFormat ofRepeatChar(char quoteChar, NSupportMode condition, NCharEscapeSet charEscapeSet, NCharEscape boundaryEscape)
```

Repeat-char strategy (SQL / Pascal / CSV).

**Parameters:**

- `quoteChar` - the single boundary character (e.g. `'\''`)
- `condition` - when to add surrounding boundary characters
- `charEscapeSet` - which additional chars to escape and how (null = none)
- `boundaryEscape` - how to encode the boundary char (null → [NCharEscape#REPEAT](#cls-NCharEscape))



---

<a id="m-NStringLiteralFormatBase-ofPrefix"></a>

#### ofPrefix

```java
public static NStringLiteralFormatBase ofPrefix(String linePrefix, String lineSuffix, NSupportMode condition, NCharEscapeSet charEscapeSet)
```

Prefix strategy: every logical line is wrapped as
`linePrefix + encodedLine + lineSuffix`.

**Parameters:**

- `linePrefix` - string prepended to every output line (empty = none)
- `lineSuffix` - string appended  to every output line (empty = none)
- `condition` - `NEVER` suppresses prefix/suffix entirely
- `charEscapeSet` - which chars to escape and how (null = none)

<a id="m-NStringUtils-replacePlaceholder"></a>

#### replacePlaceholder

```java
public static String replacePlaceholder(String text, String regexp, Function<String,String> mapper)
```

replace all placeholders in a text string with values from a given
mapper. Here is an example :
```java
String result=NMsgVarTextParser.replacePlaceholder("a${b}ad","${(?&lt;var&gt;[a-z]+)}",x->x+x);
    // result is abbad
```

**Parameters:**

- `text` - text to replace the placeholders in
- `regexp` - regular expression of the placeholder. The regexp MUST
              define the 'var' group
- `mapper` - mapper function that replaces each placeholder. When it
              returns null, no changes are made

**Returns:**

text with all placeholders replaces with values from
<code>mapper</code>



---

<a id="m-NStringUtils-replacePlaceholder"></a>

#### replacePlaceholder

```java
public static String replacePlaceholder(String text, String regexp, String varName, Function<String,String> mapper)
```

replace all placeholders in a text string with values from a given
mapper. Here is an example :
```java
String result=NMsgVarTextParser.replacePlaceholder("a${b}ad","${(?&lt;var&gt;[a-z]+)}","var",x->x+x);
    // result is abbad
```

**Parameters:**

- `text` - text to replace the placeholders in
- `regexp` - regular expression of the placeholder. The regexp MUST
               define the varName
- `varName` - the varName in the regex, defaults to
               <code>NMsgVarTextParser.DEFAULT_VAR_NAME</code> aka <code>"var"</code>
- `mapper` - mapper function that replaces each placeholder. When it
               returns null, no changes are made

**Returns:**

text with all placeholders replaces with values from
<code>mapper</code>



---

<a id="m-NStringUtils-replacePlaceholder"></a>

#### replacePlaceholder

```java
public static String replacePlaceholder(String text, Pattern regexp, String varName, Function<String,String> mapper)
```

replace all placeholders in a text string with values from a given
mapper. Here is an example :
```java
String result=NMsgVarTextParser.replacePlaceholder("a${b}ad",Pattern.compile("${(?&lt;var&gt;[a-z]+)}"),"var",x->x+x);
    // result is abbad
```

**Parameters:**

- `text` - text to replace the placeholders in
- `regexp` - regular expression of the placeholder. The regexp MUST
               define the varName
- `varName` - the varName in the regex, defaults to
               <code>NMsgVarTextParser.DEFAULT_VAR_NAME</code> aka <code>"var"</code>
- `mapper` - mapper function that replaces each placeholder. When it
               returns null, no changes are made

**Returns:**

text with all placeholders replaces with values from
<code>mapper</code>

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NToStringBuilder"></a>

## NToStringBuilder

```java
public class NToStringBuilder
```

Fluent builder for creating structured, readable, and configurable `toString()` representations of objects.
Supports auto-wrapping between single-line and multi-line modes, nested builders, primitive arrays,
optional values, conditional field inclusions, string literal formatting, and custom separators.

**Author:** thevpc

**Since:** 0.8.0

### Fields

| Modifier and Type | Field | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `private String` | `name` |  |
| `private List&lt;Entry&gt;` | `entries` |  |
| `private int` | `rowSize` |  |
| `private String` | `indentString` |  |
| `private String` | `separator` |  |
| `private Boolean` | `multiLine` |  |
| `private boolean` | `omitNulls` |  |
| `private boolean` | `omitBlanks` |  |
| `private boolean` | `omitEmpty` |  |
| `private boolean` | `omitProcessingSuppliers` |  |
| `private boolean` | `quoteStrings` |  |

```java
public NToStringBuilder()
```

Creates a new anonymous `NToStringBuilder`.

```java
public NToStringBuilder(String name)
```

Creates a new `NToStringBuilder` with the specified name.

| `NToStringBuilder` | [addIfNonEmpty(String, NOptional&lt;?&gt;)](#m-NToStringBuilder-addIfNonEmpty) | Adds the entry if the NOptional is non-null and present. |
| `NToStringBuilder` | [addIfNonEmpty(String, Object)](#m-NToStringBuilder-addIfNonEmpty) | Adds the entry if the value is not empty (string, collection, map, array, optional, blankable). |
| `NToStringBuilder` | [addIfTrue(String, boolean)](#m-NToStringBuilder-addIfTrue) | Adds the boolean field only if it is true. |
| `NToStringBuilder` | [addIfFalse(String, boolean)](#m-NToStringBuilder-addIfFalse) | Adds the boolean field only if it is false. |
| `NToStringBuilder` | [addIfNonZero(String, int)](#m-NToStringBuilder-addIfNonZero) | Adds the integer field only if it is non-zero. |
| `NToStringBuilder` | [addIfNonZero(String, long)](#m-NToStringBuilder-addIfNonZero) | Adds the long field only if it is non-zero. |
| `NToStringBuilder` | [addIfNonZero(String, double)](#m-NToStringBuilder-addIfNonZero) | Adds the double field only if it is non-zero. |
| `String` | [build()](#m-NToStringBuilder-build) | Builds and returns the formatted string representation. |
| `private String` | [buildEntry(String, Object, boolean)](#m-NToStringBuilder-buildEntry) | Formats a single entry key and value into a string. |
| `private String` | [formatValue(Object, boolean)](#m-NToStringBuilder-formatValue) | Formats a value object into its string representation according to the builder's configuration. |
| `private String` | [formatString(String)](#m-NToStringBuilder-formatString) | Fast-path string quoting. |
| `private static boolean` | [isObjectEmpty(Object)](#m-NToStringBuilder-isObjectEmpty) | Helper to test if an object is considered empty. |
| `String` | [toString()](#m-NToStringBuilder-toString) | Builds and returns the formatted string representation. |

<a id="m-NToStringBuilder-of"></a>

#### of

```java
public static NToStringBuilder of()
```

Creates a new anonymous `NToStringBuilder`.

a new `NToStringBuilder` instance



---

<a id="m-NToStringBuilder-of"></a>

#### of

```java
public static NToStringBuilder of(String name)
```

Creates a new `NToStringBuilder` with the given type/class name.

**Parameters:**

- `name` - object or type name to prefix the output with

**Returns:**

a new `NToStringBuilder` instance



---

<a id="m-NToStringBuilder-of"></a>

#### of

```java
public static NToStringBuilder of(Object obj)
```

Creates a new `NToStringBuilder` using the resolved class name of the specified object or class.
Correctly handles member nested classes (e.g. `"Outer.Inner"`), anonymous inner classes,
and array classes.

**Parameters:**

- `obj` - object whose class name to use, or `Class` instance

**Returns:**

a new `NToStringBuilder` instance



---

<a id="m-NToStringBuilder-of"></a>

#### of

```java
public static NToStringBuilder of(Class<?> clazz)
```

Creates a new `NToStringBuilder` using the resolved class name of the given class.
Correctly handles member nested classes (e.g. `"Outer.Inner"`), anonymous inner classes,
and array classes.

**Parameters:**

- `clazz` - class whose name to use

**Returns:**

a new `NToStringBuilder` instance



---

<a id="m-NToStringBuilder-resolveClassName"></a>

#### resolveClassName

```java
public static String resolveClassName(Class<?> clazz)
```

Resolves a clean, human-readable name for any class, handling top-level classes,
member nested/inner classes (e.g. `"Outer.Inner"`), local classes,
anonymous inner classes (e.g. `"Outer$anonymous"`), and array classes (e.g. `"String[]"`).

**Parameters:**

- `clazz` - the class to resolve

**Returns:**

resolved readable class name, or `null` if null

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NTruth"></a>

## NTruth

```java
public enum NTruth implements NEnum
```

Represents a four-valued logic used in composable predicates.
Values:


- TRUE: definitely true
- FALSE: definitely false
- UNKNOWN: unknown, cannot decide, known values dominate
- INDETERMINATE: unknown, can be either, propagates uncertainty



### Enum Constants

| Constant | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `TRUE` | Represents logical truth. |
| `FALSE` | Represents logical falsity. |
| `UNKNOWN` | Represents an indeterminate or unknown truth value. |
| `INDETERMINATE` | Represents unknown, can be either, propagates uncertainty |

### Fields

| Modifier and Type | Field | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `private final String` | `id` |  |

### Constructors

#### `NTruth()`

```java
NTruth()
```

N truth.


### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `String` | [id()](#m-NTruth-id) | Id. |
| `static NOptional&lt;NTruth&gt;` | [parse(String)](#m-NTruth-parse) | Parse. |
| `boolean` | [isTrue()](#m-NTruth-isTrue) | Returns true if this value is #TRUE. |
| `boolean` | [isFalse()](#m-NTruth-isFalse) | Returns true if this value is #FALSE. |
| `boolean` | [isUnknown()](#m-NTruth-isUnknown) | Returns true if this value is #UNKNOWN. |
| `boolean` | [isIndeterminate()](#m-NTruth-isIndeterminate) | Returns true if this value is #INDETERMINATE. |
| `NTruth` | [and(NTruth)](#m-NTruth-and) | Logical AND of this value with another, following four-valued logic. |
| `NTruth` | [or(NTruth)](#m-NTruth-or) | Logical OR of this value with another, following four-valued logic. |
| `NTruth` | [not()](#m-NTruth-not) | Logical NOT of this value. |
| `NTruth` | [xor(NTruth)](#m-NTruth-xor) | Returns the logical exclusive OR (XOR) of this value and another. |
| `static NTruth` | [andAll(Iterable&lt;NTruth&gt;)](#m-NTruth-andAll) | Returns the conjunction (AND) of all provided values. |
| `static NTruth` | [orAll(Iterable&lt;NTruth&gt;)](#m-NTruth-orAll) | Returns the disjunction (OR) of all provided values. |
| `static NTruth` | [andAllSuppliers(Iterable&lt;? extends Supplier&lt;NTruth&gt;&gt;)](#m-NTruth-andAllSuppliers) | Lazily evaluates the conjunction (AND) of supplied truth values. |
| `static NTruth` | [orAllSuppliers(Iterable&lt;? extends Supplier&lt;NTruth&gt;&gt;)](#m-NTruth-orAllSuppliers) | Lazily evaluates the disjunction (OR) of supplied truth values. |
| `static NTruth` | [of(Boolean)](#m-NTruth-of) | Converts a Boolean to a NTruth. |
| `Boolean` | [toBoolean()](#m-NTruth-toBoolean) | Converts this NTruth to a Boolean. |

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NTruthPredicate"></a>

## NTruthPredicate

```java
@FunctionalInterface
public interface NTruthPredicate<T>
```

A three-valued predicate returning an [NTruth](#cls-NTruth) instead of a boolean.



This is a generalization of `Predicate` suitable for situations where
a logical condition may be indeterminate.



**Since:** 0.8.x

### Method Summary

| Modifier and Type | Method | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `NTruth` | [test(T)](#m-NTruthPredicate-test) | Evaluates this predicate on the given argument. |
| `default NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [negate()](#m-NTruthPredicate-negate) | Negate this predicate. |
| `default NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [and(NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-and) | Logical AND of this predicate with another. |
| `default NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [or(NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-or) | Logical OR of this predicate with another. |
| `default NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [xor(NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-xor) | Logical XOR of this predicate with another. |
| `static NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [andAll(Iterable&lt;NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-andAll) | Compose multiple predicates using AND. |
| `static NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [orAll(Iterable&lt;NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-orAll) | Compose multiple predicates using OR. |
| `static NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [andAllSuppliers(Iterable&lt;? extends Supplier&lt;NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;&gt;&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-andAllSuppliers) | Compose multiple suppliers of predicates using AND. |
| `static NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [orAllSuppliers(Iterable&lt;? extends Supplier&lt;NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;&gt;&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-orAllSuppliers) | Compose multiple suppliers of predicates using OR. |
| `static NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [of(Predicate&lt;T&gt;)](#m-NTruthPredicate-of) | Wrap a standard boolean predicate into an NTruthPredicate (TRUE/FALSE). |
| `static NTruthPredicate&lt;T&gt;` | [constant(NTruth)](#m-NTruthPredicate-constant) | Constant predicate returning a fixed NTruth value. |

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NUnused"></a>

## NUnused

```java
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.SOURCE)
@Target({ ElementType.METHOD, ElementType.TYPE, ElementType.PARAMETER, ElementType.ANNOTATION_TYPE, ElementType.CONSTRUCTOR, ElementType.LOCAL_VARIABLE, ElementType.PACKAGE, ElementType.TYPE_PARAMETER, ElementType.TYPE_USE })
public @interface NUnused
```

Indicates that an annotated element (method parameter, field, or method)
is intentionally unused in the current implementation, preserved for API
signature compatibility or future extension.

**Since:** 0.5.4


---

<a id="cls-net-thevpc-nuts-util-NUseDefault"></a>

## NUseDefault

```java
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.RUNTIME)
@Target(ElementType.METHOD)
public @interface NUseDefault
```

Indicates that an SPI or interface implementation method delegates to
the default runtime implementation rather than providing a custom implementation.

**Since:** 0.5.4


---
